DOMAIN.APPLIED.HEALTH-MEDICINE — COMPACT CANONICAL CONTRACT

Classification: Application.
Role: healthcare delivery, EHR, Patient Twin, clinical decision support and pharmacovigilance.

Required generated state coverage:
  EHR/clinical records; PatientTwin state; clinical decision-support rules and clinical guidelines; active medication
  orders/prescriptions; and pharmacovigilance/adverse-event state. A generated layout may reorganize these roles but may
  not silently omit them.

Stable generated/runtime identities:
  SCHEMA-249A59CE57 — PatientTwin.
  SCHEMA-EAE6FFFD80 — Prescription.
  SCHEMA-04BB634ADD — AdverseEvent.

Detailed HealthState maps/lists and routine storage/UI/CRUD elaboration are generated projections. Canonical source
retains the decisions that a generator may not invent:

RegisterPatient(patient, consent) -> PatientTwin
  Requires valid consent. PatientTwin is protected under applicable privacy/security rules; plaintext PII is not
  permitted outside an authorized protected boundary.

Diagnose(patient) -> Diagnosis
  Clinical decision support may suggest a diagnosis. A causal diagnosis claim requires an applicable causal model plus
  declared assumptions; generic correlation is insufficient.

RecommendMedication(patient, medicine) -> MedicationRecommendation
  Produces a recommendation with uncertainty and contraindication/interaction checks. Recommendation != Authorization.

AuthorizeMedicationOrder(recommendation, QualifiedClinicalAuthority, ConsentBinding)
  -> GenericResult<MedicationOrder>
  Requires lawful qualified clinical authority, patient-specific checks, applicable consent, policy/law and required
  review. AI output alone never creates prescribing authority.

ReportAE(adverse_event) -> typed reporting result
  Requires immediate timestamped local capture. External reporting is jurisdiction/version/offline aware and cannot
  falsely claim compliance while delivery is unavailable.

Lifecycle semantics remain: patient data follows applicable privacy/data-lifecycle policy; medication orders retain
their ordered/dispensed/administered/completed history as applicable; adverse-event history remains provenance-bound.

Invariant references: HLT-001..004.
Dependencies: HumanCore.Privacy, Capability.Knowledge, Capability.Trust.Safety, Capability.Authority.Governance,
              Capability.Resilience.Emergency, Capability.State.TimeService, Engine.Reason, Engine.Proof.
Math/proof bindings: statistics/clinical trials, physiological models, causal inference; P2 and P4.

GSL-DISPLAY v13.0 — CERTIFICATION-BOUND DISPLAY / OCULAR SAFETY PROFILE

Status: RATIFIED SAFETY PROFILE; implementation and certification remain pending unless separate evidence exists.
Primary architecture objects: Domain.Applied.Media, Capability.Trust.Safety, Capability.Infrastructure.Hardware,
Engine.Compliance, Capability.State.TimeService, Capability.Knowledge and Capability.State.EventService.

Normative safety rules:
- The final optical stack SHALL obtain IEC 62471 Class 1 certification within Risk Group 0 or Risk Group 1 for its
  declared operating profile before unrestricted certified operation.
- A TRAIN/K0P-independent hardware limiter caps output at the certified maximum radiance/exposure envelope and responds
  within 1 ms on the qualified safety path. Human preference, software policy or model output cannot override it.
- Cumulative daily photochemical dose is tracked. Worst-case reciprocity is treated as additive until measurement and
  an accepted model establish a less conservative relation.
- Default full-certified-brightness use is at most one continuous 30-minute session per 24 hours. Multiple sessions
  require proportional derating or a separately certified exposure schedule.
- Flash content is blocked when frequency exceeds 3 Hz for duration greater than 1 second under the active profile.
- Certification, calibration, device identity, optical-path configuration, session history and dose ledger are bound to
  each safety decision and receipt.

Informative non-binding reference model:
- sustained eye brightness design target: 0.2 nits;
- peak: 100 nits for less than 100 ms per burst, maximum five bursts / 0.5 seconds total;
- estimated peak dose below 0.5 J/m² and sustained-dose estimate approximately 9 J/m² under the stated reference model;
- Micro-OLED 1920×1080 at 90 Hz and 0.5% ocular-waveguide efficiency.
These are reference engineering parameters, not universal limits or evidence of certification. The certified device,
optical geometry, spectrum, user exposure and applicable standard clauses control.

Schema references: SCHEMA-61E78E76F1 — DisplaySafetyProfile.; SCHEMA-FB7FFEEA8E — DisplayExposureLedger.;
                   SCHEMA-922037F63E — DisplaySafetyReceipt.
Invariant references: DSP-001..010.

FORMAL THREE-LEVEL RETRIEVAL/APPLICABILITY STACK
L0 — INDEX: broad lexical/semantic/type/provenance retrieval; maximizes recall and creates no authority. L1 — STRUCTURAL
RELEVANCE: match ProblemSignature, ontology, units, relations, dependencies, causality and context; rank structurally
relevant candidates. L2 — FORMAL APPLICABILITY: verify assumptions, dimensional consistency, validity scope, effects,
authority, epistemic state, resource feasibility, proof/safety requirements. UNKNOWN applicability ≠ NOT_APPLICABLE.
Only L2-applicable candidates may enter an execution/decision path requiring formal applicability.

Artifact schema reference: SCHEMA-FA17B800D1 — EvolutionProposal..

Schema references: SCHEMA-6F3B8F0DCA — ChangeSet.; SCHEMA-8218C84B43 — ImprovementCandidate.; SCHEMA-238B34DBE2 —
                   ImprovementFrontier.; SCHEMA-AB29C6F451 — DoNothingCandidate..
Improvement Comparison Contract: Every Evolution cycle MUST represent the current baseline and DO_NOTHING as explicit
candidates alongside proposed changes. Candidate dominance is evaluated only after hard constraints are satisfied. A
candidate that violates a hard constraint is infeasible regardless of objective gains. Among feasible candidates, the
ImprovementFrontier records whether each candidate dominates, is dominated by, or is incomparable with prior candidates.
Historical frontier memory MUST be retained so Evolution can detect repeated trade-offs and avoid treating previously
rejected or dominated changes as novel without new evidence.

DO_NOTHING is time- and environment-bound. Its comparison includes the expected cost/risk of inaction, external drift
and a mandatory next-review condition. Repeated DO_NOTHING wins cannot suppress bounded exploration or make an expired
baseline permanently authoritative.

Functions: [1] Discovery & Admission: ├── Sources: Engine.Research, Human UI, RES-004/005/006 alerts. ├── Gate 1 (U16):
Cryptographic authority attestation required. ├── Gate 2 (U03): Token-bucket rate limit (prevents flooding). ├── Gate 3
(U01): Vector-clock sequencing + atomic locks. └── Output: ConceptCandidate.

[2] Anti-Nonsense Triage (P-107): ├── Step 2a: PBC Assessment (Problem, Benefit, Cost/Risk). ├── Step 2b: 5 Rejection
Tests (Problem, Counterfactual, Capability-Delta, Complexity, Anti-Self-Amp). ├── Step
2c (U02): Fuzzy semantic hashing + AST normalization. └── Output:
TriageCandidate (PASS | FAIL | DEFER).

[3] Routing & Applicability (P-108): ├── Step 3a: Independent Architecture-Object Discovery (P-108A). ├── Step 3b:
Concept Discovery. ├── Step 3c: Rule Discovery. ├── Step 3d (U18): Conservative Guardrail Filters. ├── Step 3e (U15):
Hierarchical error tracing. └── Output: RoutingPath + GranularErrorCode.

[4] Improvement Frontier & Mandatory DO_NOTHING Comparison: ├── Materialize each admissible proposal as an
ImprovementCandidate. ├── Include Current_System and DO_NOTHING as explicit comparison candidates.
├── Compare feasible candidates against the persistent
ImprovementFrontier. ├── Record DOMINATES / DOMINATED_BY / INCOMPARABLE relations. ├── Preserve theoretical and
empirical objective bounds and historical outcomes. └── If no candidate dominates DO_NOTHING under the acceptance
policy, DO_NOTHING remains admissible.

[5] Static Impact & Blast Radius (U06): ├── Automated static analysis on proposed ChangeSet. ├── Formally bound the
declared blast_radius under the analyzed dependency and execution model. └── If unproven → REJECT /
REVISE.

[6] Formal Verification (Engine.Proof): ├── Generate Proof Obligations. ├── Discharge via dedicated Evolution Worker
Pool (U09) → strict CPU quota.
├── If FAIL → Revision loop (back to [2]). └── Output: ProofCertificate.

[7] Implementation & Rollback (U11): ├── Generate ChangeSet. ├── U11:
Prepare atomic two-phase journaled snapshot for rollback. └── Output:
ChangeSet (Status: IMPLEMENTED).

[8] Validation (GSSCC): run independent validation under a ValidationCircuitBreakerProfile whose timeout/WCET bound is
qualified by operation class, hardware, proof budget and safety requirements. Timeout/resource exhaustion returns the
typed terminal state; no universal 100 ms value is implied. Failure follows the declared rollback/compensation path.

[9] Global State Commit (U17): ├── Enforce atomic two-phase commit across all Capability.Infrastructure.Fabric read
replicas. ├── Update semantic version (historical baseline → prior baseline). ├── U07: Bind new version baseline hash to
hardware OTP root. └── Output: New Baseline.

[10] Recursive Coverage & Oscillation Checks: ├── RES-004/005/006
(Audit). U13: Exponential backoff on feedback loops. ├── U04:
Multi-dimensional matrix cycle detection. ├── U19: Non-maskable hardware interrupt → Capability.Authority.Governance.
└── Output: CoverageGapCandidate → feeds back to Step [1].

Update Protocol: Proposal: CANDIDATE → ACCEPTED → SPECIFIED → PROVEN →
IMPLEMENTED → VALIDATED → ESTABLISHED | REJECTED. ChangeSet: PROPOSED →
VERIFIED → DEPLOYED → ROLLED_BACK.

Invariant references: EVO-001..006, REJ-001, EVO-007, RES-004, EVO-009..013.
Dependencies & Math: Read: All components (observes non-blocking), Capability.Knowledge, Engine.Proof,
Capability.Authority.Governance, Engine.Research, Engine.Reason, Capability.Compute.Runtime, Capability.Trust.Security,
Engine.Compliance. Write: Capability.Knowledge, Capability.State.EventService, Capability.Compute.Execution,
Capability.Knowledge.Curation. Math: M0 (Termination - P5 rank), M3 (Epistemic Debt scoring), M1 (Fixed-point analysis),
M4 (Complexity). Proofs: P5, P6, P8, P9, P15, P17, P18, P19, P20. Additional contract checks: ImprovementFrontier
dominance consistency; DO_NOTHING comparison completeness.

33.0 — SELF-AUDIT & RECURSIVE PATCH MECHANISM

STATUS: NORMATIVE UPGRADE TO Engine.Evolve (EVOLUTION) ROLE: Full-design self-audit, bounded recursive patching and
independent adversarial validation.

PRINCIPLE
 “No code may be deployed unless it has been checked against the entire technical design, patched
 recursively until compliance, and passed an adversarial review.”

“Entire technical design” means the candidate is routed against the complete version-bound canonical registry: all
canonical typed architecture objects, current Core Objects/relations/types, effects, dependencies, invariants,
proof/evidence obligations, rights, K0P, authority, DesignEpoch/DesignBinding, schemas, interfaces, profiles and
applicable contextual rules. Each item receives APPLICABLE, CONDITIONALLY_APPLICABLE, NOT_APPLICABLE or UNKNOWN with an
explanation; it does not mean executing irrelevant tests blindly.

State extension
EvolutionState += {
 self_audit_queue: Queue<SelfAuditRequest>,
 active_audits: Map<SelfAuditID, FullDesignConformanceAudit>,
 patch_sessions: Map<PatchSessionID, RecursivePatchSession>,
 adversarial_reviews: Map<ReviewID, AdversarialReviewReport>,
 frozen_verifier_binding: FrozenVerifierBinding,
 canonical_design_registry_root: Hash,
 max_recursive_patch_depth: UInt = 3,
 self_audit_certificates: AppendOnlyList<SelfAuditCertificate>
}

Schema references: SCHEMA-A291D54A25 — SelfAuditRequest.; SCHEMA-BA967485E2 — FullDesignConformanceAudit.;
                   SCHEMA-559709D2B0 — ConformanceFinding.; SCHEMA-2BFBBE04C8 — RecursivePatchSession.;
                   SCHEMA-3D908D74A9 — AdversarialReviewPlan.; SCHEMA-3F37C2E16B — AdversarialReviewReport.;
                   SCHEMA-4C065EE3CE — SelfAuditCertificate.; SCHEMA-A5CA6A42D6 — AuditScopeClosure..

FINITE AUDIT-CLOSURE CONTRACT
"Entire technical design" refers to the complete finite, version-bound canonical registry and the machine-computed
applicable closure for the declared artifact/deployment scope—not to enumeration of every possible mathematical object
or future theory. The request declares a candidate scope, but cannot make itself complete by selecting a convenient
subset.

Engine.Dependency/Compliance computes AuditScopeClosure from artifact effects, interfaces, dependencies, active
profiles, schemas, theories, contexts, rights, invariants and authority bindings. Every registry item is included or has
a typed NOT_APPLICABLE/exclusion justification. Closure root, algorithm/version, termination/resource contract,
unknowns and a completeness proof/receipt bind the audit. Missing or unproven closure is INCOMPLETE/UNKNOWN and cannot
produce a full-design certificate.
Invariant references: AUD-SCOPE-001..006.

Full pipeline
 generated or modified artifact
 → immutable source/build receipt
 → isolated Evolution branch
 → bind DesignEpoch, DesignBinding, environment and complete canonical registry root
 → executable recursive routing/applicability audit across the full design
 → compiler/type/effect/dependency/authority and Structural Integrity checks
 → invariant/proof/evidence/safety/security/privacy/rights/conformance matrix
 → findings
 → if patchable and depth < 3: generate candidate patch on child branch
 → dependency/blast-radius analysis + regression suite
 → rerun the full audit against the patched artifact
 → repeat until compliant, blocked, or depth/budget termination
 → independent adversarial review under declared threat model
 → Engine.Proof, Engine.Compliance, frozen-verifier and authority gates
 → SelfAuditCertificate
 → GTF Prepare → Validate → Commit
 → deployment only within certificate scope
 → runtime monitoring, invalidation and recovery.

Recursive patch rule
 - Patches never overwrite the audited baseline; each is an immutable branch/commit.
 - Every patch recomputes affected dependency/blast-radius closure and reruns the complete registry
 applicability audit; a local fix can create a distant violation.
 - Patch generation and verification are causally/organizationally separated under CND-001…CND-009.
 - Maximum automatic recursive depth is 3. Depth exhaustion, oscillation, unresolved fatal/critical
 findings, invalid trust state or resource exhaustion automatically abandons/reverts the candidate
 branch to the last accepted state, preserves all evidence, releases the deployment pipeline and
 escalates for independent repair; it is never declared pass.
 - A patch may reduce scope or choose DO_NOTHING/REJECT when compliant repair is unavailable.

Adversarial review rule
 “Survives all attacks” means all applicable attacks in the declared, versioned threat model and
 AdversarialReviewPlan were executed within the stated coverage/resource boundary and left no
 unresolved fatal/critical finding. It does not mean survival against every logically possible or
 future unknown attack. INCONCLUSIVE/RESOURCE_UNKNOWN cannot become SURVIVED by wording or vote.

Status machine
 REQUESTED → BOUND → AUDITING → FINDINGS
 → PATCHING → REAUDIT ↺ (depth ≤ 3)
 → ADVERSARIAL_REVIEW → FORMAL_CONFORMANCE → CERTIFIED → PREPARED → COMMITTED → MONITORED.

Terminal/exception states
 REJECTED | HALTED | ESCALATED | INCONCLUSIVE | RESOURCE_UNKNOWN | QUARANTINED.
Accepted exceptions require explicit authority, bounded scope/expiry, do not cover hard Constitution/K0P violations, and
remain visible in the certificate.

Self-audit integrity
 The Self-Audit mechanism, registry resolver, patch controller and adversarial orchestrator cannot
 certify changes to themselves. Such changes require a frozen prior verifier, independent
 implementation, immutable test corpus, DesignEpoch transition and external certification authority.

Invariant references: SAP-001..010.
Integration
 Primary: Engine.Evolve, Engine.Proof, Engine.Compile, Capability.Platform.Development, Engine.Compliance,
 Engine.Reason, Engine.Dependency, Capability.Trust.Security, Capability.Knowledge, Capability.State.EventService,
 Engine.Transition, Capability.Compute.Runtime.
 Hard gates: Capability.Trust.Safety, HumanCore.Constitution, HumanCore.Privacy, Engine.Policy,
 Capability.Authority.Governance, K0P.

creating new top-level architecture objects or independent lifecycle engines.

ENGINE.EXPLANATION — DETAILED SPECIFICATION

35.0 GSL-EXPLAIN — UNIVERSAL EXPLANATION ENGINE

STATUS: NORMATIVE ENGINE/CAPABILITY UPGRADE
CLASSIFICATION: Meta / Epistemic / Human-Comprehensibility ROLE: Produce faithful, audience-appropriate, challengeable
explanations from Garden’s typed semantic, evidential, causal, proof, decision, authority and transition graphs.

PRINCIPLE
 Explanation reveals the best available trace; it does not invent a missing trace, create evidence,
 raise epistemic status, authorize action, or certify the system that generated it.

Question taxonomy
 WHAT What is the object/claim/decision/state?
 WHY What reasons, evidence, rules or causes support it?
 HOW What process, mechanism or sequence produced it?
 WHY_NOT Why was an alternative rejected, blocked or not selected?
 WHAT_IF What is predicted under an explicit intervention/change?
 WHEN What time/order/validity/expiry applies?
 WHERE What spatial/contextual scope applies?
 WHO Which entities/agents/roles participated?
 AUTHORITY Who authorized/delegated the consequential action, under what scope?
 EVIDENCE What observations/evidence/proofs support or refute it?
 UNCERTAINTY What is unknown, disputed, stale, unidentifiable or model-dependent?
 ALTERNATIVES What feasible/Pareto/dissenting alternatives remain?
 COUNTERFACTUAL What would differ under a declared historical/causal intervention?
 CHANGE What changed between versions/states, why, and with what effects?

Internal State (GSL typed)
Schema references: SCHEMA-E0185BF219 — ExplainState.; SCHEMA-1800D7A315 — ExplanationRequest.; SCHEMA-6D20D5DFD9 —
                   ExplanationPlan.; SCHEMA-02E4AEE291 — ExplanationGraph..
ExplanationNode.kind =
 CLAIM | EVIDENCE | OBSERVATION | ASSUMPTION | RULE | PROOF_STEP | CAUSE | EVENT | ACTION |
 DECISION | RECOMMENDATION | AUTHORIZATION | COMMITMENT | EXECUTION | OUTCOME | ALTERNATIVE |
 DISSENT | UNKNOWN | CONFLICT | RESOURCE_BOUND | REDACTION

ExplanationEdge.kind =
 SUPPORTS | REFUTES | DERIVED_FROM | ASSUMES | PROVES | CAUSES | PRECEDES | AUTHORIZES |
 DELEGATES | BLOCKS | SELECTS | REJECTS | SUPERSEDES | CONFLICTS_WITH | WOULD_CHANGE |
 DEPENDS_ON | AFFECTS | REDACTED_BY

Schema references: SCHEMA-0BE89EC80A — CausalChain.; SCHEMA-A8098AC22E — DecisionChain.; SCHEMA-F9A80CA68A —
                   WhatIfScenario.; SCHEMA-22533CC37B — UncertaintyAnnotation.; SCHEMA-A6706765A1 — ExplanationBundle.;
                   SCHEMA-1C0E31AB36 — ExplanationReceipt..
Primary pipeline
 ExplanationRequest
 → authenticate/authorize and bind privacy/consent
 → normalize question and classify question types
 → resolve target, version, time, branch and scope
 → construct ExplanationPlan
 → retrieve semantic/evidence/proof/causal/decision/authority/dependency graphs
 → compute causal, historical and counterfactual chains where requested/applicable
 → preserve alternatives, dissent, uncertainty, gaps and redactions
 → answer the actual question first
 → adapt language/depth/locale/accessibility without semantic promotion
 → apply output-view redaction after generation as well as source-view redaction before generation
 → semantic/contextual privacy-leak, faithfulness, traceability and unsupported-statement checks
 → ExplanationBundle + immutable ExplanationReceipt
 → optional comprehension check / corrected explanation.

Functions
 Explain(request) → ExplanationBundle

 ExplainWhy(target, context) → ExplanationBundle
 ExplainHow(target, context) → ExplanationBundle
 ExplainWhyNot(target, alternative, context) → ExplanationBundle
 ExplainWhatIf(target, intervention, context) → ExplanationBundle
 ExplainAuthority(action_or_decision) → ExplanationBundle
 ExplainEvidence(claim) → ExplanationBundle
 ExplainUncertainty(target) → ExplanationBundle
 CompareExplanations(targets_or_alternatives) → ExplanationBundle
 TraceToSource(statement) → ProvenanceTraversal
 Summarize(bundle, compression_profile) → ExplanationBundle
 Adapt(bundle, audience_locale_accessibility) → ExplanationBundle
 VerifyFaithfulness(bundle, source_graphs) → FaithfulnessReport
 RecordUnderstanding(bundle, subject_response?) → UnderstandingRecord

What-if / counterfactual discipline
 A WHAT_IF result is a PredictionArtifact unless independently observed after an authorized intervention.
 It declares the model, assumptions, fixed/changed variables, uncertainty, causal level, and comparator.
 Counterfactual explanation does not rewrite history and does not attribute present knowledge to a
 historical actor. Dangerous interventions remain simulation-only unless separately authorized.

Update protocol
 REQUESTED → AUTHORIZED → PLANNED → GRAPH_BOUND → GENERATED → FAITHFULNESS_CHECKED →
 ADAPTED → DELIVERED → CHALLENGED | UNDERSTOOD | SUPERSEDED.

 Failure/terminal states:
 REJECTED_UNAUTHORIZED | INSUFFICIENT_CONTEXT | UNKNOWN | INCONCLUSIVE | RESOURCE_UNKNOWN |
 PRIVACY_REDACTED | UNSUPPORTED_EXPLANATION_REJECTED.

 Context.LocaleCulture, Engine.Transition, Engine.Evolve.

 retention/privacy policy.

ENGINE.POLICY — DETAILED SPECIFICATION

Classification: Meta / Operational Role:
evaluates and enforces typed PolicyArtifacts using PA v1.0a / Decision Algebra. Policy does not own constitutional,
legal, safety or domain truth; it composes valid policy content within authority/scope.

Internal State (GSL Typed):
Schema references: SCHEMA-CA9D2E5B64 — PolicyState.; SCHEMA-9ED6288244 — PolicyArtifact.; SCHEMA-4E9C5D83B9 —
                   PolicyContext.; SCHEMA-208C94398B — PolicyDecision.; SCHEMA-BBC79BC452 — AcceptanceVector.;
                   SCHEMA-562936379C — PolicyEvaluationTrace..
Functions:
Evaluate(context: PolicyContext) → PolicyDecision
 Precondition: context is typed, authority/scope/time bindings are explicit and current.
 Postcondition: returns one Decision-Algebra result plus all approvals/obligations/conflicts/uncertainty.
 Contract: Constitution/rights, K0P, law and valid authority remain independent hard gates; policy evaluation cannot
 create
 authority or convert UNKNOWN/CONFLICT into PERMIT.

Enforce(decision: PolicyDecision, action: Action) → Outcome
 Precondition: decision is fresh, applicable, and all required approvals/obligations/admission gates are satisfied.
 Postcondition: PEP permits, blocks, defers or invokes the declared safe fallback.
 Contract: a PolicyDecision alone never bypasses Constitution/rights, K0P, authenticity, authorization, consent or
 admission-critical proof/evidence requirements.

UpdatePolicy(candidate: PolicyArtifact, authority: AuthorityRef) →
 ACCEPTED | REJECTED | BLOCKED | CONFLICT | INCONCLUSIVE
 Precondition: candidate owner/scope/version/provenance are valid and the authority is competent for that policy scope.
 Postcondition: accepted changes create a new immutable policy version and invalidate affected caches/decisions.
 Contract: update cannot weaken higher hard gates or silently drop simultaneously required approval obligations.

Audit(context_or_decision_ref) → PolicyEvaluationTrace
 Returns the immutable reconstructible evaluation trace for the declared scope/version.

PA v1.0a mandatory composition semantics:
 - AND_D and OR_D remain distinct unless equivalence is formally established for the declared fragment.
 - Multiple simultaneously applicable approval requirements compose as an explicit requirement set; none is silently
   lost.
 - Conflicting applicable policies remain CONFLICT or follow a registered ResolveStrategy; runtime objects cannot invent
   a
   new resolution rule.
 - SAFE_FALLBACK is conditional on its declared failure/uncertainty path and is never an unconditional permit.
 - Policy content remains owned by its substantive authority; Algebra.Policy owns universal composition semantics.

Update Protocol:
 PolicyArtifact: DRAFT → PROPOSED → REVIEWED → APPROVED → ACTIVE → SUPERSEDED | REPEALED | STALE.
 PolicyDecision: CURRENT → STALE/BLOCKED when any bound policy/context/authority/evidence/DesignEpoch dependency
 changes.

                   ResearchTrace.; SCHEMA-68A2DA3E4A — ConceptCandidate.;

hypothesis's epistemic status.

Precondition: The applicable diminishing-return threshold is reached and no protected research floor blocks archival.
Postcondition: Preserves the complete claim/evidence/falsification/search history and removes the hypothesis from the
ordinary active queue. UNKNOWN remains UNKNOWN; archive status is scheduling state, not truth state.

Reactivate(request: HypothesisReactivationRequest) → GenericResult<Bool>
Requires novel evidence, a materially new test or measurement method, changed assumptions/model/context, a periodic
low-cost review trigger, or authorized human nomination followed by ordinary resource/safety review. Human nomination
creates consideration, not automatic priority or epistemic promotion.

Epistemic diminishing-return policy protects safety-critical, rights-relevant, rare-event, neglected/minority and
long-horizon hypotheses from mechanical starvation. Absence of new evidence is interpreted relative to search coverage,
test power, expected evidence-arrival rate and feasibility; it is never automatically evidence of falsity.

Update Protocol: Research: OBSERVE → HYPOTHESIZE → SEARCH → EXPERIMENT →
VALIDATE → UPDATE.

ENGINE.REASON — DETAILED SPECIFICATION

Classification: Epistemic / Cognitive /
Theory-Selection / Meta-Reasoning.
Status: NORMATIVE — UNIFIED REASONING, THEORY & FORMAL-PROOF SYSTEM.

PRINCIPLE
Engine.Reason chooses, composes and controls reasoning strategies. Domain.Mathematics supplies mathematical structures
and formal mathematical objects. Engine.Proof checks formal proofs and discharges proof obligations.
Capability.Knowledge supplies epistemic state, claims and evidence. Engine.Research acquires new empirical evidence and
may generate candidate theories. Engine.Search retrieves candidate theories, patterns, evidence and analogies.
Engine.Explanation renders reasoning structures for humans. Engine.Policy, HumanCore.Constitution and
Capability.Trust.Safety constrain consequential recommendations/actions.

Reasoning ≠ Proof ≠ Evidence ≠ Authorization. Recommendation ≠ Authorization. Formal proof ≠ empirical validation.

50.1 UNIFIED REASONING SYSTEM

Engine.Reason
│
├── Problem Formalization
│ └── ProblemSignature
│
├── Reasoning Mode Registry
│ ├── Four Foundational Inference Families
│ └── Specialized / Composed Modes
│
├── Theory Registry
│ └── TheorySignature + TheoryDependencyGraph
│
├── Assumption Registry
├── Evidence Graph
├── Inference / Proof Graph
├── Mode Selection
├── Mode Composition
├── Falsification / Challenge
├── Uncertainty Propagation
├── Resource / Termination Control
├── Proof Handoff
├── Explanation Handoff
└── Meta-Reasoning

50.2 FOUR FOUNDATIONAL INFERENCE FAMILIES

1. ABDUCTION
 Inference to a candidate/best explanation.

2. ARGUMENTATION
 Dialectical reasoning using attack/defense structures, including Dung
 Grounded / Preferred / Stable semantics.

3. DEDUCTION
 Necessary consequence under declared premises, axioms and formal calculus.

4. EMPIRICAL INDUCTION
 Generalization from observations/evidence with quantified uncertainty.

Critical distinction:
 EMPIRICAL INDUCTION ≠ MATHEMATICAL INDUCTION.
Mathematical induction is a deductive FORMAL_PROOF strategy, not empirical generalization.

50.3 FIRST-CLASS REASONING MODE REGISTRY

Top-level ReasoningMode values:
 DEDUCTIVE
 INDUCTIVE_EMPIRICAL
 ABDUCTIVE
 ARGUMENTATIVE
 ANALOGICAL
 DEFAULT_NONMONOTONIC
 PROBABILISTIC
 CAUSAL
 COUNTERFACTUAL
 TEMPORAL
 SPATIAL
 CONSTRAINT
 DECISION
 ETHICAL_NORMATIVE
 GAME_THEORETIC
 OPTIMIZATION
 PLANNING
 EXPLANATORY
 ADVERSARIAL
 THEORY_REASONING
 FORMAL_PROOF
 MODELING
 PREDICTIVE
 META

PROBABILISTIC subprofiles include:
 BAYESIAN | LIKELIHOOD | GRAPHICAL_MODEL | STATISTICAL

The current registry therefore contains 24 top-level modes. Counts are descriptive rather than constitutional: a future
mode refinement does not change the typed architecture registry.

Cache validity MUST bind at least:
 DesignEpoch
 KnowledgeSnapshot
 EnvironmentCommitment
 DependencyClosure
 AssumptionSet
 FormalSystemVersion
A cache hit whose binding is stale is invalid.

50.5 CORE ARTIFACTS

Schema references: SCHEMA-3625DC2AA3 — ProblemSignature.; SCHEMA-80D86FE2A7 — TheoryStatus.; SCHEMA-0798799B08 —
                   ReasoningTrace.; SCHEMA-0C0C4C33EF — EvidenceSet.; SCHEMA-06B084334D — UnknownSubtype..
50.6 EXISTING THEORY-ORCHESTRATION FUNCTIONS — RETAINED

Formalize(query: Query) → ProblemSignature
 Precondition: query GSL-typed.
 Postcondition: well-formed ProblemSignature.

Retrieve(signature: ProblemSignature) → Set<Theory>
 Uses Engine.Search L0/L1/L2 retrieval/applicability path.
 Structural match is primary; keyword similarity alone is insufficient.

Falsify(theory: Theory, budget: ResourceLimits) → FalsificationResult
 Searches for counterexamples/assumption failures.
 Survival ≠ proof.

Compose(theories: Set<Theory>) → Theory
 Requires compatibility of variables, assumptions, units, scope, uncertainty,
 boundary conditions and validity domains.

ManageEvidence(evidence: Evidence) → EvidenceSet
 Evidence remains provenance-bound; LLMs cannot manufacture Evidence.

50.7 DEDUCTION ENGINE

Deduce(
 premises: Set<GSL_Expr>,
 query: GSL_Expr,
 calculus: FormalSystem,
 budget: InferenceBudget
) → DeductionResult

Preconditions:
 - premises and query are well-formed GSL expressions;
 - the formal system/calculus is declared;
 - assumptions and applicability scope are explicit.

Supported deductive strategies include:
 propositional reasoning; first-order reasoning; predicate reasoning;
 syllogistic reasoning; forward chaining; backward chaining;
 modus ponens; modus tollens; contrapositive; contradiction;
 proof by cases; equivalence; constraint solving; SAT; SMT;
 theorem-prover handoff where appropriate.

Pipeline:
 Premises + Query
 → Logical Formalization
 → Consistency / well-formedness check
 → Select calculus / solver
 → Forward/backward reasoning and/or proof search
 → Proof / refutation / terminal status

Schema references: SCHEMA-CFCB2666D1 — DeductionResult..
Semantics:
 DEDUCED = query follows in the declared formal system.
 REFUTED = negation of query follows in the declared formal system.
 NOT_DEDUCED = permitted only when the declared fragment/procedure is complete
 for the question and establishes non-entailment.
 INVALID = malformed/inconsistent formalization under the declared rules.
 UNKNOWN = timeout, undecidable case, incomplete calculus, missing assumptions,
 resource exhaustion or unresolved formalization.

No proof found ≠ NOT_DEDUCED in a generic bounded/incomplete reasoning system. DEDUCED is logical consequence
conditional on premises; it is not empirical truth. Deduction cannot amplify the epistemic status of uncertain premises
merely by being valid.

50.8 EMPIRICAL INDUCTION ENGINE

Induce(
 observations: Set<Observation>,
 hypothesis_space: HypothesisSpace,
 induction_profile: InductionProfile
) → InductionResult

Supported profiles:
 enumerative induction; statistical generalization; Bayesian induction;
 pattern induction; trend inference; population inference;
 causal induction; hypothesis generalization.

Pipeline:
 Observations
 → quality/provenance/independence checks
 → candidate pattern generation
 → support + contradiction analysis
 → sampling/selection-bias analysis
 → uncertainty estimation
 → out-of-distribution analysis
 → scope determination
 → InductionResult

Schema references: SCHEMA-3B90D63E61 — InductionProfile..
No universal confidence formula is mandated. The historical simple profile support/(support+contradiction) is only a
suitable special case for narrowly defined binary settings. General induction is governed by the Mathematical
Representation Policy and domain-appropriate estimators.

A counterexample is not automatic rejection unless the hypothesis claims universal, exceptionless behavior. Statistical
laws may legitimately contain expected exceptions.

Schema references: SCHEMA-BD8B56830E — InductionResult..
InferenceMeasure may represent Probability, Distribution, Interval, LogOdds, LogEvidence, Confidence, SupportMeasure or
another semantically declared measure. Representation ≠ semantic meaning. Empirical induction produces
support/uncertainty, never formal necessity.

50.9 MATHEMATICAL INDUCTION & FORMAL PROOF STRATEGY REGISTRY

Mathematical induction is deductive formal proof, not empirical induction.

Flow:
 Engine.Reason selects ProofStrategy
 → Domain.Mathematics supplies mathematical structures/induction principles
 → Engine.Proof machine-checks proof obligations
 → ProofCertificate

ProofStrategy leaf strategies currently include:

Classical logical:
 DIRECT
 CONTRAPOSITIVE
 CONTRADICTION
 CASE_ANALYSIS
 EXISTENCE
 UNIQUENESS

Computational / algebraic:
 EQUATIONAL
 ALGEBRAIC
 RECURSIVE
 FIXED_POINT

Mathematical induction:
 INDUCTION_WEAK / ORDINARY
 INDUCTION_STRONG / COMPLETE
 INDUCTION_STRUCTURAL
 INDUCTION_WELL_FOUNDED
 INDUCTION_COURSE_OF_VALUES
 INDUCTION_MUTUAL
 INDUCTION_NESTED
 INDUCTION_LEXICOGRAPHIC
 INDUCTION_TRANSFINITE

Coinduction:
 COINDUCTION_BISIMULATION
 COINDUCTION_GREATEST_FIXED_POINT

Automated:
 AUTOMATED_SAT
 AUTOMATED_SMT
 THEOREM_PROVER

Ordinary induction:
 P(0) ∧ (P(n) → P(n+1)) ⇒ ∀n∈ℕ, P(n)

Strong induction:
 (∀k≤n, P(k)) → P(n+1), together with the required base condition(s).

Structural induction applies to recursively generated structures including:
 GSL ASTs; proof terms; expression trees; recursive schemas;
 dependency structures; process structures; branch histories.

Well-founded induction generalizes to a declared relation with no infinite descending chain. Transfinite induction
applies over a declared well-order. Coinduction establishes properties of potentially infinite/co-recursive structures
through bisimulation/greatest-fixed-point reasoning.

A successful mathematical induction proof yields FORMAL PROVEN status relative to the declared axioms, definitions,
induction principle and formal system after Proof checks. It does not by itself establish that empirical premises/models
about the physical world are true.

50.10 ABDUCTION

Observation
 → candidate explanatory hypotheses
 → assumption + scope analysis
 → explanatory comparison
 → falsification attempts
 → best current candidate(s)

ABDUCTED = best available explanatory candidate under the declared comparison.
It never means TRUE, PROVEN or VERIFIED merely because it explains the observation.

50.11 ARGUMENTATION

Claim
├── supports
├── attacks
├── rebuttals
├── defeaters
└── defenses

Supported semantics include Dung Grounded, Preferred and Stable semantics.
ARGUED = accepted/surviving under the declared argumentation framework.
ARGUED ≠ FACT.

50.12 ANALOGICAL REASONING

Source system
 → structural mapping
 → candidate correspondence
 → difference analysis
 → applicability check
 → transferred hypothesis

Structural similarity ≠ proof. Analogical transfer creates a candidate relation requiring independent validation.

50.13 DEFAULT / NON-MONOTONIC REASONING

Supports defeasible rules and explicit exceptions, e.g.:
 Bird(x) normally → Flies(x)
 Penguin(x) → exception → ¬Flies(x)

Conclusions carry supporting defaults, checked exceptions, defeaters, retraction conditions and validity scope. New
evidence may legitimately retract a previous conclusion; this is not an error in non-monotonic reasoning.

50.14 PROBABILISTIC / BAYESIAN REASONING

General form:
 prior state + evidence + dependency structure → posterior state

For Bayesian probability semantics:
 P(H|E) = P(E|H)·P(H)/P(E)

Constraints:
 - probability semantics must actually apply;
 - Mathematical Representation Policy controls representation;
 - Probability, LogOdds, LogEvidence, Interval, Distribution, Tensor, etc.
 are representations/types with declared semantics, not interchangeable universal scores;
 - correlated evidence cannot be treated as independent merely because it came from
 multiple records/models/sources.

50.15 CAUSAL REASONING

Association
 → causal hypothesis
 → SCM / causal graph
 → assumption validation
 → intervention reasoning
 → causal estimate

Supports intervention semantics such as P(Y | do(X=x)). Causal claims MUST identify causal structure, confounders,
assumptions, identification method, intervention semantics and scope appropriate to the claim. Correlation alone cannot
create causal status.

50.16 COUNTERFACTUAL REASONING

Observed world + causal/model state + alternative intervention
 → counterfactual trajectory.

Directly supports Historical Counterfactual Optimization, Prediction, Evolution, Decision analysis and Incident
reconstruction. Counterfactual conclusions inherit the uncertainty/limitations of the model from which they are
generated.

50.17 TEMPORAL & SPATIAL REASONING

Temporal:
 before/after; during; since; until; always; eventually; valid-at; expired-at;
 superseded-at; causal ordering; temporal obligations.

Spatial:
 inside/outside; containment; adjacency; intersection; distance; direction;
 route; reachability; coordinate transformation; topological relation.

These consume existing TIME and SPACE semantics and do not create new ontology.

50.18 ADVERSARIAL / FALSIFICATION REASONING

Candidate
 → assume candidate may be wrong
 → attack assumptions/boundary conditions/evidence/composition
 → search counterexamples
 → test adversarial variants.

Survival means only NO COUNTEREXAMPLE FOUND WITHIN DECLARED SEARCH, never proof.

50.19 DECISION REASONING

Beliefs + candidate actions + predicted consequences + values + risks + constraints + resources
 → Recommendation.

Possible form:
 a* = argmax_a EU(a)
subject to Constitution, Rights, Safety/K0P, Authority, Law, Policy, Resources and explicit epistemic uncertainty.

Recommendation ≠ Authorization.

50.20 ETHICAL / NORMATIVE REASONING

FACT = what evidence indicates is.
VALUE = what is valued/preferred.
RIGHT = protected constitutional constraint.
LAW = governed legal requirement.
POLICY = operational rule.
DECISION = recommended action.

IS ⇏ OUGHT without an explicit normative premise. Reasoning may relate these categories but may not silently convert one
into another.

50.21 GAME-THEORETIC / MULTI-AGENT REASONING

Models agents, strategies, information, beliefs, payoffs, incentives, coalitions, adversaries, equilibria, coordination
and mechanism design. Relevant to Economy, Governance, Security, Agent, Evolution and Earth-branch coordination. An
equilibrium is strategic stability under assumptions; it does not establish ethical legitimacy.

50.22 OPTIMIZATION REASONING

Objective + decision variables + feasible set + constraints + uncertainty + baseline
 → candidate optimum / Pareto set.

Where applicable, mandatory comparison includes Current_System, DO_NOTHING and candidate alternatives. Optimal ≠ Safe.
Optimal ≠ True. Optimal ≠ Authorized.

50.23 PLANNING REASONING

Current State → Goal → candidate actions → preconditions/effects
 → temporal/resource constraints → prediction → contingencies → Plan.

Supports classical, hierarchical, temporal, contingent, resource-constrained and multi-agent planning. Execution remains
Capability.Compute.Execution, subject to authority/safety.

50.24 EXPLANATORY REASONING

Prediction asks: What happens? Explanation asks: Why did it happen?

Observed outcome
 → causal/procedural lineage
 → relevant mechanism
 → assumptions + alternatives
 → ExplanationArtifact.

Engine.Reason determines explanatory structure; Engine.Explanation renders it for humans.

50.25 THEORY REASONING — GENERAL THEORY ENGINE

Engine.Reason supports first-class:
 theory discovery; theory invention; retrieval; formalization; applicability;
 comparison; falsification; composition; specialization; generalization;
 contradiction; equivalence; approximation; supersession; dependency;
 revalidation; selection; retirement.

Schema references: SCHEMA-72F1762E05 — TheorySignature..
Theory operation ownership:
 Discovery/Invention → Engine.Research + Engine.Reason, producing candidate theories.
 Retrieval → Engine.Search.
 Applicability/Comparison → Engine.Reason.
 Falsification → Engine.Reason + Engine.Research where experiments are needed.
 Composition → Engine.Reason, compatibility required.
 Contradiction/Equivalence→ Engine.Reason + Engine.Proof where formalizable.
 Dependency → Engine.Dependency.
 Revalidation → Engine.Reason + Capability.Knowledge + Continuous Epistemic Revalidation.
 Supersession → Engine.Reason proposal + Capability.Knowledge lineage + normal GTF governance.

Theory invention creates a CANDIDATE theory, never established truth.

50.26 THEORY STATUS SYSTEM

Theory status is separate from inference status.
High-level lifecycle/status vocabulary:
 ESTABLISHED | DERIVED | PROPOSED | EXPERIMENTAL | SPECULATIVE | REJECTED

A theory separately carries formal_status, empirical_status, application_status, scope, freshness and provenance.
"Deduced using Theory X" does not imply "Theory X is empirically correct."

50.27 META-REASONING

Meta-reasoning decides how Garden should reason.

It asks:
 - Which reasoning modes apply?
 - Which theories are relevant/applicable?
 - How deep should reasoning go?
 - Which models/tools/solvers are appropriate?
 - Which modes can safely compose?
 - What would falsify the result?
 - What evidence is missing?
 - When should reasoning stop, defer or escalate?

Triggers include novelty, UNKNOWN/CONFLICTED outcomes, resource pressure, mode disagreement, changed theory
dependencies, and Safety/Constitution requirements.

Examples:
 Arithmetic → Deduction.
 Mathematical theorem → Deduction + Formal Proof + mathematical induction if suitable.
 Clinical causal query → Causal + Bayesian + Evidence + Counterfactual + Adversarial.
 Engineering design → Deduction + Modeling + Optimization + Prediction + Counterfactual + Adversarial verification.
 Unknown phenomenon → Abduction + Empirical Induction + Theory Discovery + Experiment Design.
 Conflicting legal case → Deduction + Argumentation + Temporal + Ethical/Normative reasoning.

Output: ReasoningPlan + ReasoningTrace.

50.28 MODE COMPOSITION

Modes are composable but cannot silently amplify each other.

Example:
 Observation
 → Abduction
 → Candidate Hypothesis
 → Empirical Induction
 → Supported Pattern
 → Causal Analysis
 → Candidate SCM
 → Deduction
 → Conditional consequence.

Formal necessity conditional on uncertain premises ≠ unconditional certainty. If valid modes materially disagree, status
remains CONFLICTED or UNKNOWN until resolved. No averaging merely to force a single answer.

Reason Composition Contract:
Theories/modes composed by Engine.Reason MUST provide compatible UncertaintyPropagationProfile semantics. If required
profiles are absent, incompatible or outside declared error bounds, composition status MUST be UNKNOWN and MUST NOT be
promoted as verified.

50.29 INFERENCE STATUS ≠ EPISTEMIC STATUS

InferenceStatus =
 DEDUCED | REFUTED | NOT_DEDUCED |
 INDUCED_SUPPORTED | INDUCED_WEAK | INDUCED_CONTRADICTED |
 ABDUCTED | ARGUED | INVALID | UNKNOWN

EpistemicStatus remains orthogonal, including:
 PROVEN | VERIFIED | SUPPORTED | UNVERIFIED | CONTRADICTED | RETRACTED | UNKNOWN/subtypes.

Example:
 InferenceStatus = DEDUCED
 EpistemicStatus = SUPPORTED
because the inference is formally valid but an empirical premise is only SUPPORTED.

50.30 UNIVERSAL INFERENCE RESULT

Schema references: SCHEMA-76AE49974C — InferenceResult..
50.31 REASONING PIPELINE — UPA PROFILE

The current normalized process:
 FORMALIZE → RETRIEVE → FALSIFY → COMPOSE → CHECK → MANAGE → REASON
is retained as lineage but normalized as a Universal Process Algebra profile:

PROBLEM
 → FORMALIZE
 → CLASSIFY
 → RETRIEVE theories/evidence
 → SELECT reasoning modes
 → BUILD ReasoningPlan
 → EXECUTE modes
 → FALSIFY / CHALLENGE
 → COMPOSE compatible results
 → VERIFY formal obligations
 → PROPAGATE uncertainty
 → CHECK epistemic/scope/dependency status
 → TERMINATE / DEFER / ESCALATE
 → InferenceResult
 → ClaimValidationPlan where promotion/consequential use is intended
 → Proof / Research / Simulation-Test / Legal-Authority / Human-Review paths as required
 → Explain / Recommend / ResearchGap / UNKNOWN.

50.31.1 REASONING-TO-VALIDATION ROUTER

Schema references: SCHEMA-6CC4CB8E94 — ClaimValidationPlan.
Every inference or candidate claim intended for promotion, consequential recommendation or action receives a typed
ClaimValidationPlan. Routing is many-to-many rather than a false Proof-versus-Research binary:

  FORMAL            → Engine.Proof checks entailment, proof obligations or model consistency.
  EMPIRICAL         → Engine.Research acquires/tests observations and evidence.
  BOTH              → Formal and empirical paths are independently required; neither substitutes for the other.
  SIMULATION_TEST   → Qualified simulation, benchmark, property test or domain validation.
  LEGAL_AUTHORITY   → Law/Policy/Constitution resolves applicability and authorization, not empirical truth.
  HUMAN_REVIEW      → Qualified human/domain/governance review where judgment or legitimate authority is required.
  UNROUTABLE_UNKNOWN→ Clarify, quarantine, defer or escalate; never silently promote.

Routing predicates consume claim kind, intended use/promotion, assumptions, falsifiability/testability, formalizability,
domain, risk, authority, evidence gap, resources and context. A plan may select several paths with dependency order,
acceptance criteria, terminal behavior and reconciliation rules. Engine.Reason owns routing; each destination retains
its own epistemic and authority boundary.
Invariant references: VAL-ROUTE-001..006.

50.32 CONTINUOUS REVALIDATION / THEORY DEPENDENCY INTEGRATION

New evidence or a changed upstream theory triggers:
 Continuous Epistemic Revalidation
 → affected claims/theories discovered
 → TheoryDependencyGraph marks dependent theories/inferences REVALIDATION_REQUIRED
 → affected cached InferenceResults invalidated
 → applicable modes re-run
 → CONFIRMED / STRENGTHENED / WEAKENED / REFINED / CONTRADICTED / UNKNOWN.

Invalidation is impact-indexed, incremental and priority-bounded. A REFUTED upstream theory immediately quarantines
consequential affected outputs, but recomputation proceeds in batches/lazily from immutable snapshots rather than
synchronously traversing the entire graph or crashing active Runtime contexts. Runtime may use only an unaffected
last-valid binding or enter a declared conservative degradation.

Reasoning is therefore living, dependency-aware and DesignEpoch-bound.

50.33 EARTH CANONICAL BRANCH INTEGRATION

Local branch theory/proof/observation
 → BranchChangeManifest
 → Earth Frontier
 → Engine.Reason: comparison, deduction, induction, analogy, causal/counterfactual analysis,
 adversarial challenge and discriminating-evidence identification
 → Research/Proof
 → MERGE | REMAIN_LOCAL | REJECT | UNRESOLVED.

Convergence/votes/popularity may nominate a candidate but cannot create truth.

50.34 INVARIANT REFERENCES — REASONING FAMILY

Canonical definitions: Annexure A.
Invariant references: RSN-001..010, RSN-011..014 RESERVED, RSN-015..021, INF-001..024.

 HumanCore.Constitution,
 Capability.State.TimeService, Capability.Authority.Governance, Engine.Search, Engine.Evolve, Engine.Explanation,
 Engine.Dependency,
 Engine.Policy, Engine.Research, Domain.Society.Economics, HumanCore.DelegatedAgent.
Write:
 Capability.State.EventService, Capability.Knowledge, and governed Research/Evolution requests.

Math families used include:
 M0 proof theory, formal logic, Hoare/type systems;
 M1 graph/order/algebra/category/refinement/concurrency;
 M3 probability/statistics/information/causal inference/decision/game theory;
 M4 optimization/complexity/solver selection.

Relevant proof obligations include P2, P3, P5, P6, P7a/P7b, P8, P12, P14, P18 and other obligations induced by the
actual reasoning plan and ObligationGraph.

RPM/PPM v2.0 — FUNCTIONCONTRACT SEMANTIC CLOSURE

The six v14.8.3 regulation functions inherit the ordinary REG-FUNCTION-001 effect, authority, failure, dependency,
resource, DesignEpoch, EnvironmentCommitment, provenance and explanation fields. Exact executable contracts:

instantiate_reactive_regulation_model
  Input : RegulationStateDefinitionRef, RegulationAgentPoolProfileRef, RegulationValidatorProfileRef,
          RegulationDynamicsProfileRef, CouplingCompositionProfileRef?, Context/DesignEpoch bindings.
  Output: ReactiveRegulationModelProfile | INVALID | INCOMPLETE | UNKNOWN.
  Rule  : state/operator/geometry/parameter/dependency closure is explicit; construction != proof != empirical validity
          != Safety != authority.

assess_regulation_regime
  Input : ReactiveRegulationModelProfileRef, typed state/trajectory/observations, classifier/profile and context.
  Output: RegulationRegimeAssessment.
  Rule  : regime classification is a derived, uncertainty-bearing assessment; it is not ontology, Evidence, hazard
  truth,
          Safety or authority.

assess_regulation_stability
  Input : regulation model/profile, exact stability property, assumptions, method/proof/evidence/simulation refs and
  scope.
  Output: RegulationStabilityReceipt.
  Rule  : the receipt states the exact mathematical/engineering claim; simulation alone is neither formal proof nor
          Safety assurance.

instantiate_proactive_regulation_model
  Input : RegulationStateDefinitionRef, RegulationAsymmetryProfileRef, RegulationFidelityProfileRef,
          RegulationDynamicsProfileRef, RegulationClockModeProfileRef?, CouplingCompositionProfileRef?, context.
  Output: ProactiveRegulationModelProfile | INVALID | INCOMPLETE | UNKNOWN.
  Rule  : typed H(z), parameters, fidelity and coupling must resolve; model construction != HazardPredicate != FET
          admission != authority.

evaluate_adaptive_fidelity_gate
  Input : ProactiveRegulationModelProfileRef, current/requested fidelity, approximation/error state, resources,
  assurance
          requirements and context.
  Output: FidelityLevel | FORCE_FULL | BLOCKED | UNKNOWN.
  Rule  : lower fidelity cannot waive mandatory Constitution/rights/K0P/HSA/Safety/authority/AAP/proof/evidence gates.

transition_regulation_fidelity
  Input : ProactiveRegulationModelProfileRef, current/target fidelity, TimeService state, update ownership,
  queue/barrier
          state and transition preconditions.
  Output: AdaptiveFidelityReceipt | BLOCKED | FAILED.
  Rule  : transition is atomic; failure cannot duplicate, lose or reorder state updates or silently change authority.

8.6 STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY / LLM PREFLIGHT
VII-C — STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY / LLM PREFLIGHT GATE

C.1 STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY GATE

Before any artifact — source code, plan, SQL query, legal document, CAD model, robotic motion script, medical plan, or
other execution/knowledge artifact — is committed to Capability.Knowledge or passed to Capability.Compute.Execution, it
MUST pass SIG-P1…SIG-P4.

Proof references: SIG-P1..SIG-P4.

Failure: If SIG-P1…SIG-P4 fails, emit StructuralGap, reject the artifact, and route the failure trace through
Engine.Reason/Engine.Evolve for correction before re-entry.

C.2 SECTION 5.2 — STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY GATE

The SIG is a deterministic validation layer and primary filter for LLM/AI- generated artifacts entering
Capability.Compute.Runtime and Capability.Knowledge.

Example SIG-P1: let mut run_len = 1; loop { run_len += 1; } If run_len is
never read, reject with StructuralGap(SIG-P1).

Example SIG-P2: let i = 10; let result = i - 20; Reject unsafe underflow and
suggest guarded/saturating subtraction.

Example SIG-P3: pub fn new(policy: Policy) when Policy is private: reject StructuralGap(SIG-P3); expose a valid public
type.

The structural-coverage gate rejects dead fields/variants where the lifecycle requires structural use.
Proof references: SIG-P1..SIG-P4.

Failure protocol: 1. Structure Gap with exact precondition and line location. 2. Pass Gap to Engine.Reason and
Engine.Evolve. 3. Do not fall back to probabilistic LLM output. 4. Correct through proof or human-in-the-loop review
where required.

8.7 PROCEDURAL SEMANTIC MEDIA PROFILE
VII-D — PROCEDURAL RECIPE / SEMANTIC MEDIA LAYER

D.1 SECTION 5.3 — CUBE PROCEDURAL RECIPE LAYER

Domain.Applied.Media may represent generated media through semantic procedural recipes rather than retaining raw pixels
wherever the application permits.

SceneRecipe: category: Tavern | Forest | Apartment parameters: {style,
weather, timeOfDay} generator: ProceduralGeneratorRef

CharacterRecipe: bodyProportions, clothingRules, voiceProfile, walkingStyle

AnimationRecipe: type: Walk | Run | Idle solver: IKSolverRef keyframes:
[KeyframeRef]

PhysicsRecipe: type: Cloth | Fire | Rain preset: PresetRef parameters:
{density, speed}

WorldRecipe: roadGenerator, buildingGenerator, populationDistribution

StoryRecipe: genre: Mystery | Romance structure: {victim, suspects,
reveal}

Pipeline: Raw Video -> Phi Bridge (Extract Semantics) -> Match existing
Recipes -> Cache Hit: reuse Recipe -> Cache Miss: generate Recipe via
GPU -> Proof verification -> Capability.Knowledge

Storage-reduction claims remain implementation/benchmark claims and require validation; no fixed percentage is promoted
to a universal proven invariant.

8.8 LEGAL SEMANTIC SUBSTRATE

Purpose: represent, version, test and analyze legal material without confusing formal consistency with legal truth,
institutional authority, admissibility or a court decision. Primary architecture objects: GardenCore.Semantics.GSL,
Domain.Mathematics, Engine.Proof, Capability.Knowledge, Capability.Authority.Law, Capability.Knowledge.Curation,
Capability.Authority.Governance, Engine.Compliance, Engine.Compile, Capability.State.EventService, Engine.Dependency,
Engine.Policy and Engine.Reason.

E.1 CAPABILITY-STATUS AND FORMALIZATION BOUNDARY

Reading/retrieval
 ≠ structural parsing
 ≠ semantic formalization
 ≠ verified encoding
 ≠ legally authoritative interpretation
 ≠ deployed automated gap detection.

Schema references: SCHEMA-BA71CA01DC — LegalAnalysisReceipt..
A manually or conversationally produced gap report is SIMULATED_ANALYSIS unless an implemented engine, machine-readable
corpus, reproducible solver trace and verification evidence establish a stronger status. The overall Garden
implementation remains pending; no architectural description may imply that a deployed national legal scanner already
exists.

E.2 LEGAL SEMANTIC VERSION CONTROL

Schema references: SCHEMA-942D7DBC21 — LegalArtifact.; SCHEMA-A01FD0E9C8 — TestPattern.; SCHEMA-3B4A072E51 —
                   CoverageProfile..
No universal percentage proves adequate legal coverage. Activation requires the risk-weighted semantic coverage declared
by the applicable profile, with every uncovered high-consequence path explicit.

Functions

Commit(artifact: LegalArtifact) -> LegalCommitReceipt
 Requires authoritative source/provenance, typed schema, authority, applicable tests, dependency impact,
 constitutional and rights checks, and the required SMT/human review. An SMT pass alone is insufficient.

Diff(artifact_id, version_a, version_b) -> SemanticDelta
 Returns textual, semantic, temporal, jurisdictional, deontic, dependency and test differences with
 preserved provenance.

TraceOrigin(predicate_or_block) -> OriginTrace
 Identifies the introducing source/commit and subsequent changes. It records provenance, not culpability.

Branch(jurisdiction_or_context, parent_commit) -> LegalArtifact
 Preserves parent provenance and dependencies while declaring the exact contextual delta.

E.3 BOUNDED SMT LEGAL SOLVER

Schema references: SCHEMA-242CE5CFBF — SMTLegalState.; SCHEMA-348B826153 — GapQuery..
Enumerate(domain, resource_contract) -> GapEnumerationReport
 For an explicitly finite Boolean predicate space, bounded enumeration may cover all 2^|S|
 combinations. Integer, real, temporal, quantified, exception-rich and open-world legal domains use
 typed symbolic constraints, partitions or bounded model checking rather than pretending they are a
 finite Boolean cube. Coverage, exclusions and solver completeness remain explicit.

VerifyPatch(block, existing_blocks, global_invariants) -> PatchVerificationReport
 Checks syntax, types, satisfiability, deontic/temporal/jurisdictional interactions, constitutional
 constraints and affected tests. An UNSAT core is diagnostic evidence requiring interpretation; it does
 not mechanically prove that the proposed legal text is invalid in every legal reading.

IncrementalCheck(changed_block) -> ImpactAnalysisReport
 Rechecks the transitive affected dependency closure plus all designated global invariants and
 common-mode constraints. Incrementality cannot hide a global effect outside the local closure.

Proof reference: LEGAL-SMT-P25.

E.4 AUTOMATED LEGAL CI/CD

Official source monitoring
 → content-addressed change detection
 → parsing
 → candidate formalization
 → ambiguity and trust assessment
 → dependency/global impact analysis
 → tests and SMT analysis
 → qualified human review where required
 → authority/governance gate
 → semantic commit
 → deployment/advisory release
 → continuous monitoring and revalidation.

Invariant reference: EVO-014.

E.5 FORMALIZATION TRUST

Schema references: SCHEMA-A89ED91715 — Predicate..
VerificationLevel =
 V0_RAW_TEXT | V1_PARSED | V2_OPERATIONALIZED | V3_MACHINE_VERIFIED | V4_FORMALLY_PROVEN

The operationalization degree tau(p) estimates confidence in the declared translation/formalization under its evidence
and review conditions. It is not the probability that the legal proposition is true, just, authoritative or
court-adopted. Low tau triggers ambiguity preservation and review; human approval does not magically make a poor
formalization true.

Invariant reference: LANG-010.

E.6 AI-ASSISTED FORMALIZATION

AI systems may extract candidate blocks, citations, definitions, exceptions, temporal scopes and dependencies at scale.
Deterministic parsers, cross-model checks, tests, SMT analysis, source comparison and qualified human experts review the
risk-appropriate subset.

No fixed AI/human percentage is normative. A measured ratio such as 95/5 may be tested as a pilot target, but review
intensity follows ambiguity, novelty, legal consequence, jurisdiction, model performance and independent quality
evidence. Human review is a responsibility and evidence channel, not automatic truth.

E.7 HUMAN REVIEW PROFILE

Schema references: SCHEMA-7F6E0DFCAB — HumanReviewProfile..
Timelines and quorums are configurable legal/governance parameters, not universal constants. If review expires,
high-impact action remains blocked or enters the legally defined safe state; bounded low-risk continuity may use the
intersection of clearly valid permissions. Emergency procedures must be fast enough for the emergency and cannot become
a permanent constitutional bypass.

E.8 LEGAL-USE AND ADMISSIBILITY BOUNDARY

Garden output is a traceable analysis, claim, model, test result or recommendation. It is not automatically law,
evidence, admissible material, advocacy, a judgment or authority to act. Use in legal practice depends on current
applicable law, institutional rules, competent professionals and the deciding tribunal.

Where external use is contemplated, Garden preserves source authenticity, chain of custody, reproducibility, method
disclosure, limitations, competing interpretations, human responsibility and challenge rights. Human authentication
alone does not establish truth or admissibility.

E.9 STAGED IMPLEMENTATION PROFILE

1. Bounded pilot corpus: source ingestion, semantic version control, tests and reproducible solver traces.

2. Larger coherent corpus: dependency graph, contradiction/gap analysis and qualified review operations.

3. Institutional advisory twin: case-law/enforcement models, monitored use and external validation.

4. Cross-domain legal twin: only after prior stages meet proof, accuracy, rights, security, governance and
 operational evidence gates.

Each stage is advisory and bounded unless applicable law and independently validated governance authorize a stronger
role. Business/funding strategy is a separate non-normative project document.

Invariant references: LEGAL-001..015, LEGAL-SMT-P25, LANG-010, EVO-014.

8.9 GSL-BOOTSTRAP / TWO-FLEET / MODEL ROUTING
VII-F — GSL-BOOTSTRAP / TWO-FLEET ARCHITECTURE

F.1 GSL-BOOTSTRAP

Garden may be bootstrapped from zero through an external heterogeneous LLM fleet. This is an internal lifecycle
protocol, not a new Garden component.

Six phases: 1. Planning 2. Foundation 3. Building 4. Hardening 5. Handover 6. Operation

The external fleet builds and hardens the initial system. Handover requires Garden to demonstrate self-hosting
capabilities: it can verify itself, compile itself, route work through its authorized mechanisms, and perform the
declared self-audit functions. After successful handover, the external fleet steps back.

F.2 TWO-FLEET DISTINCTION

Bootstrap Fleet: External/pre-Garden heterogeneous fleet used before Garden is self-hosting.

Garden Fleet: Internal/post-Garden authorized model/reasoning fleet used for operation, research, evolution,
verification and routing subject to Garden authority.

The two roles are not interchangeable merely because both contain models.

F.3 FLEETROUTER / MODELREGISTRY

Engine.Reason internal state includes:

ModelRegistry: provider model_name version weights_hash runtime_version capability_score

CapabilityScorer dimensions: reasoning_depth domain_expertise speed cost_efficiency reliability

FleetRouter: Exploit: 90–95% Explore: 5–10% Exploration remains within the declared capability envelope.

Model != Authority. Model selection occurs only from an authorized set; routing cannot create authority.

Ensemble modes: SINGLE SEQUENTIAL PARALLEL ENSEMBLE DEBATE CROSS_VERIFY

F.4 PHYSICAL WORLD MAPPING

Home -> OS, Energy, Security, Agent Observe: temperature, motion, doors
Act: lights, locks, HVAC

City -> Mobility, Energy, Governance Observe: traffic, air, power Act:
traffic lights, grid, transit

Body -> Health, Safety Observe: biometrics Act: authorized
devices/reminders

Vehicle -> Mobility, Safety, Network Observe: GPS, LiDAR Act: brakes,
steering, throttle

Factory -> Industry, Safety, Hardware Observe: machine sensors Act:
PLCs, robotic arms

Grid -> Energy, Safety, Distributed Observe: frequency, load Act:
breakers, generation

Economy -> Economy, Governance Observe: transactions, market Act:
authorized UBI/tax mechanisms

Environment -> Governance, Carbon Observe: weather, climate Act:
controls, conservation

F.5 EPISTEMIC DEBT

Capability.Knowledge contains:

Schema references: SCHEMA-083717EE84 — EpistemicDebt..
If total_debt_score exceeds the declared threshold, Engine.Evolve MUST trigger a mandatory ImprovementCycle through
Engine.Research/Engine.Reason. DO_NOTHING remains a first-class candidate in that cycle.

F.6 IMPROVEMENTFRONTIER DOMINANCE

Every candidate is compared against DO_NOTHING as the mandatory stability baseline. Dominance states: DOMINATES
DOMINATED_BY
INCOMPARABLE

Hard constraints are checked first. Infeasible candidates are rejected. A candidate cannot be accepted merely because it
is better than another proposal; it must satisfy the DO_NOTHING comparison and the acceptance policy. Historical
frontier and dominance outcomes remain retained for lineage and anti-regression analysis.

F.7 EXTERNAL STANDARDS MAPPING

Engine.Compliance supports source-authenticated, versioned, jurisdiction- and scope-bound assessment against
applicable standards, regulations, guidance, codes, certification schemes and specifications. Examples are not
presumed applicable; applicability is determined per [T-COMPLIANCE-STD].

Canonical trace: ExternalNormativeInstrument -> RequirementInstance -> NormativeRequirementBinding ->
GardenConformanceAssessmentReceipt. Evidence may be prepared for an external authority, but only that authority's
authenticated act may be recorded as ExternalCertificationBinding. Assessment, admissibility and certification
remain distinct.

F.8 NAMING / SCOPE CLARIFICATIONS

Reality Layer is explicitly the Reality layer and asks “What happened?”.

Domain.Mathematics provides formal representations/operators for mathematical theories required by Garden operations; it
does not claim to contain every mathematical theory.

GardenCore.Semantics.GSL maps human languages to GSL through Context.LocaleCulture and the USI (Universal Semantic
Interlingua) reference from GSL-UNIVERSAL.

G.9 RECURSIVE INCIDENT LEARNING LOOP

Detect -> Capture -> Contain -> Preserve -> Reconstruct -> Classify ->
Analyze -> Root Cause -> Generalize -> Generate Candidates -> DO_NOTHING
-> Verify -> Replay -> Adversarial Test -> Regression -> Canary ->
Deploy -> Monitor -> Validate -> Commit -> Learn -> Proactively Scan ->
Audit -> Repeat

The system learns: specific incident + causal mechanism + generalized failure class + detection signature + mitigation
condition + regression test.

It does not merely memorize attacker identity, specific input, exploit string, or timestamp.

G.10 FAILURE-PATTERN LEARNING

For every verified significant failure: Incident -> Causal
Reconstruction -> Root Cause -> Failure Pattern -> Generalized
Conditions -> Detection Rule -> Mitigation Rule -> Regression Artifact.

Reusable rule form: IF capability C + state transition X + context Y + missing condition Z THEN classify as prohibited
transition and block/quarantine.

G.11 Engine.Proof PATCH COMPOSITION

VerifyPatch(change_set) -> ProofCertificate | CounterExample

CompositionProof(A o B) = ComponentProof(A) AND ComponentProof(B) AND
InterfaceProof(A,B) AND SharedInvariantProof(A,B) AND ResourceCompatibilityProof(A,B) AND
TemporalCompatibilityProof(A,B) AND FailureCompositionProof(A,B) AND AssumptionCompatibilityProof(A,B)

Fixing the original incident alone is insufficient for deployment.

G.12 Capability.Compute.Execution ATTACK REPLAY / CP1

State: attack_replay_library: Map[IncidentID, AttackReplay]

Schema references: SCHEMA-9AB50E0935 — AttackReplay..
